## Key Technical Decisions & Fixes
- Async database stack using `sqlalchemy[asyncio]` and `greenlet`.
- Server-rendered Jinja2 templates using Starlette-compliant `TemplateResponse`.
- Admin UI driven by vanilla JS `fetch()` polling to dedicated async API endpoints, strictly avoiding hardcoded business data.

## Decisions Recorded (2026-10-12)
- Admin Brands and Forms are admin-managed modules, each with a "+" button to create entries.
- A form is a shared, named template. Many jobs can use one form (`jobs.form_id`), and a job uses at most one. This REPLACES the earlier strict 1:1 decision.
- The applicant picks a job from the posting on the website and answers that job's form.
- For now there is ONE form, the "General Form", used by the crew and store jobs. Role-specific forms (e.g. "IT Recruitment") come later; the model already supports them. Nothing is seeded: the General Form is created with the "+" button.
- Jobs are attached to a form from the form's own page (`PUT /forms/{form_slug}/jobs/{job_id}`). `jobs` has no role-category field, so "crew and store" is chosen by the admin, not automatically.
- Questions belong to the form: `job_questions.form_id` replaces `job_id` (table name kept for now).
- Components are a fixed set of field types: Yes/No, Multiple choice A-D (four options written by the admin) and Open ended (free text, collected only, never scored).
- Answer points (the admin cheat sheet): each Yes/No or A-D answer choice carries admin-set points to a BRAND (Pares Retiro, Sandwich Guy, Sapporo) or to FAIL. Stored in the new `answer_points` table.
- FAIL points REPLACE `expected_answer`. Score = percent of scored questions (Yes/No and A-D) whose chosen answer has no FAIL row. Open ended is excluded from the divisor. A hard-requirement question whose chosen answer has a FAIL row sets the fail flag and status FAILED. `pass_threshold` and `pooling_threshold` work as before.
- Suggested fit is brand-level: brand points are summed per application (`applications.brand_points`). The brand with the highest total above 0 is the suggestion. A tie shows all tied brands with a flag like "suited for both ...". No brand points means no flag. The flag never changes the score or status.
- ASSUMPTIONS awaiting confirmation: Open ended answers award no points; every scored question must be answered to submit.
- `parameters` table is kept as LEGACY and `job_questions.parameter_id` becomes nullable. Retiring it is a separate confirmed step.
- The form editor saves as you go (one call per action: create, rename, add, edit, delete, reorder question). Question type is fixed after creation. Deleting a question with applicant answers returns 409. Renaming a form keeps its slug.
- A form's submissions are the applications for the jobs that use it (`GET /api/v1/admin/applications?form_id=`).
- Resume upload stays a fixed step of every application. No File upload component in this version.
- Apply wizard layout reference: progress bar, question heading, question text, options, Previous/Next (wireframe provided by user).
- `display_order` and the `slug` columns are in SCHEMA.md and `app/models.py` and are committed (2451ad7).
- Docs are current for these decisions: docs/SCHEMA.md (119 lines) and docs/API_CONTRACT.md (183 lines, section 3 marked PLANNED).
- Admin page routes (decided 2026-10-12): Dashboard `/admin/home`; Applicants list `/admin/applicants` and detail `/admin/applicants/{applicant-UID}`; Brands `/admin/brands/{brand-UID}`; Forms `/admin/forms/{form-UID}`. Page URLs use UIDs, not names or slugs; slug columns stay. This replaces `{brand-name}`/`{form-name}` from the v4 preamble and the `{form_slug}`/`{application_id}` page-route text in the docs (the contract's forms page route was reconciled on 2026-10-13; the brand and applicant page routes still say `{brand_slug}` and `{application_id}`).
- Applicants list shows full name, address, age and gender; every other form field shows only when the admin opens an applicant. One "Full Name" column is assumed (the wireframe shows first and last name).
- Applicants module stays a placeholder shell for now. Its fields come from form answers once forms exist; `Applicant` has only first_name, last_name, email and phone.
- Forms creation is built first so it can be tested. The Applicants page waits.

## Decisions Recorded (2026-10-13)
- Schema-change mechanism: option A. Plain SQL files in `migrations/NNN_*.sql`, applied with docker exec into bte_careers_db, non-destructive and idempotent where possible. Applied so far: 001 (`forms` table with unique `ix_forms_slug`), 002 (nullable `jobs.form_id`, FK to `forms`, `idx_jobs_form_id`), 003 (`forms.archived_at`), 004 (`job_questions` form columns), 005 (`jobs` posting columns) and 006 (`idx_jobs_brand_created`, 2026-10-14).
- Admin Forms page URL uses the form id: `/admin/forms/{form-UID}` (confirmed again 2026-10-13).
- Applicant flow, served by bte_webapp on :8000 for now: job landing page `/careers/jobs/{job-UID}` (job UID is `jobs.id`) shows the instructions; the form page is `/careers/jobs/{job-UID}/forms/{form-UID}`. The job travels in the path because many jobs share a form, and the server must check that the form belongs to that job (`jobs.form_id`). These routes are not yet in API_CONTRACT.md.
- Deployment layout: website on 10.0.10.37:8086 and webapp on 10.0.10.37:8000 (same VM, separate containers). Later plan, OUT OF SCOPE while local-only: website at bigtimeempire.com fetching job data from the webapp, webapp admin at ats.bigtimeempire.com/admin. The apply flow uses relative URLs so that move needs no code change.
- Security incident (2026-10-12/13): GitGuardian flagged the fallback DB password in docker-compose.yml. Cause: `DB_PASSWORD` was unset in `.env`, so the live DB ran on the stock default, and Postgres 5432 was published on all interfaces. Fixed: DB role password rotated; `DB_PASSWORD` is required in docker-compose.yml (no fallback); `app/database.py` has no `DATABASE_URL` fallback; 5432 is bound to 127.0.0.1. Committed in ab9bae3. Postgres trusts loopback connections inside its container, so password checks must go over the network (`-h db`). Remaining: mark the GitGuardian alert resolved from its dashboard, and add `DB_PASSWORD` to `.env.example`.
- Admin forms endpoints are keyed by form id (`/api/v1/admin/forms/{form_id}/...`) and the page route is `/admin/forms/{form_id}`; recorded in API_CONTRACT.md on 2026-10-13 (section 3 still PLANNED). The slug stays a response field and is used only in public paths.
- Row counts 2026-10-13: jobs, job_questions, applications and answers are all empty, so the `job_questions` reshape needs no backfill. Remaining schema work is HELD until the question editor step: migration 003 (job_questions: form_id, question_type, options, nullable parameter_id, idx_jq_form_order), 004 (answer_points and its index), 005 (applications.brand_points, answers.is_passed nullable, idx_answers_application_id), and dropping `job_id` and `expected_answer` last with explicit confirmation. The drop breaks `GET /api/v1/public/jobs/{job_id}/questions` and `POST /apply` until they are rewritten for forms.
- Form Builder modal shipped (admin.html, FORMS "+", committed cab1a44): editable form name, drag-and-drop element palette (Yes/No, Open-ended, Multiple Choice -- Checkbox excluded by decision), drop zone is visual-only for now (dragged elements are not yet persisted as questions -- no question-text/options/answer-points UI exists yet). Save calls `POST /api/v1/admin/forms` with the name, shows a Bootstrap toast on success, and refreshes the sidebar.
- Bug found and fixed 2026-10-13: the inline `<script>` in admin.html originally ran before the modal/toast markup existed in the DOM (script tag preceded that markup in source order), so `document.getElementById(...).addEventListener` threw on page load and silently broke the Save button after a fresh reload. Fixed by wrapping the modal-dependent JS in a `DOMContentLoaded` listener.
- `GET /api/v1/admin/metadata/sidebar` now reads the `forms` table instead of the legacy `parameters` table (resolves the Known Mismatch recorded 2026-10-12, see below).

## Known Mismatches (verified 2026-10-12)
- The `forms` table and `jobs.form_id` are live (migrations 001 and 002, applied 2026-10-13). `answer_points` does not exist yet. UPDATE: migration 004 added `form_id`, `question_type` and `options` to `job_questions` and made `job_id`, `parameter_id` and `expected_answer` nullable (see the Save session log below); dropping `job_id` and `expected_answer` is still held.
- RESOLVED 2026-10-13: `JobQuestion` in `app/models.py` was aligned with the table in the Save step (`form_id`, `question_type`, `options`).
- The matching engine in `POST /apply` (app/routers/public.py, lines 98-258) scores with `expected_answer` (line 182). It must be rewritten for answer points, required answers and brand points, as its own contract-first step.
- `GET /api/v1/public/jobs/{job_id}/questions` returns no question type or options.
- `app/main.py` serves `/admin`, `/admin/archive` and, as of this session, `/admin/forms/{form_id}` (title, Edit button and form builder wiring; submissions, bulk delete and export are still placeholders -- see the Session Logs below). `/admin/home`, `/admin/brands/...` and `/admin/applicants/...` are still not implemented.
- The contract lists admin application endpoints (list, detail, status patch, resume) that are not in `app/routers/admin.py`.
- Route param naming differs: the v4 preamble says `{UID}`, the contract says `{application_id}`.
- RESOLVED 2026-10-13: the sidebar `forms` list now reads the `forms` table (app/routers/admin.py), not the legacy `parameters` table.
- Bugs in `POST /apply` (not fixed): a rejected job lookup leaves an orphaned PDF on disk; an unanswered hard-requirement question never sets the fail flag; invalid `answers` JSON and unknown question ids are silently ignored. The contract now requires 422 for these.
- Hardcodes: brand `code` is `name[:3].upper()` with a "BTE" schema default; `consent_agreed` defaults True and `terms_version` defaults "v1.0" in app/models.py; in `POST /apply` the 5 MB limit, the default `/data/resumes` path (docker-compose.yml:27 mounts `./data/resumes` to `/app/data/resumes`; whether an env var overrides the default is UNVERIFIED), the 8192 chunk size, the status strings, and a score of 100 for a form with no questions.
- Cosmetic duplicates (committed in 2451ad7): `Parameter, Parameter` and `SidebarFormItem, SidebarFormItem` imports in admin.py; `slug: str` twice in SidebarBrandItem and SidebarFormItem in schemas.py.
- Indexes in SCHEMA.md but not in app/models.py: `idx_jq_form_order`, `idx_jobs_form_id`, `idx_answer_points_question_id`, `idx_answers_application_id`.
- Dashboard (app/templates/admin.html): the Dashboard, Applicants and brand sidebar links are still `href="#"` (form links point to `/admin/forms/{id}` since 2026-10-13). The FORMS "+" now opens the Form Builder modal (2026-10-13); the BRANDS "+" is still a plain span, so nothing opens there yet.
- Dashboard brand cards read `summaryData.brand_matches` (`name`, `matches`), but the contract says `brand_summaries` (`brand_name`, `top_matches_count`). Which side is wrong is UNVERIFIED (the dashboard/summary code in admin.py has not been read).
- Dashboard pass rate shows 0% with "0/1" while every count is 0. A denominator of 1 looks like a fallback value (UNVERIFIED, code not read).
- Security: admin.html puts brand and form names into `innerHTML` unescaped in the sidebar fetch code. This needs escaping before the "+" create buttons exist.
- Docs are ahead of code: SCHEMA.md and API_CONTRACT.md section 3 have no model or endpoint behind them yet.
- The 8 original tables (answers, applicants, applications, brands, job_questions, jobs, parameters, status_counts) were created outside the repo and have no baseline, so a fresh db still cannot be rebuilt from the repo. Changes since 2026-10-13 live in `migrations/` (001 forms, 002 jobs.form_id); `app/database.py` only builds the engine.
- RESOLVED 2026-10-13: the `DATABASE_URL` fallback in `app/database.py` and the `DB_PASSWORD` fallbacks in docker-compose.yml are removed (see the security incident under Decisions Recorded 2026-10-13). Fixed non-secret defaults remain for `DB_USER` and `DB_NAME` in compose. Still UNVERIFIED: `RESUME_STORAGE_PATH` is read in public.py line 127 (default `/data/resumes`); whether compose sets it.
- `Applicant` (models.py line 35) has only first_name, last_name, email and phone: no age, gender or address.
- Cosmetic in app/main.py: unused `CORSMiddleware` import and `from app.routers import public` imported twice.

- Open (Structure handoff): archive field for forms (archived_at vs is_archived), applicants and brands (brands and applicants tables not checked for an archive field, UNVERIFIED); rename and archive endpoints; archive count and list endpoints; the sidebar query must exclude archived forms (`select(Form)` in admin.py has no filter today). Add `/admin/archive` to the v4 preamble section 3 route list.

## Admin layout rules (decided 2026-10-13)
- Every admin module uses the shared sidebar. No page composes its own sidebar markup, sidebar CSS or sidebar JS.
- STANDING RULE (user, explicit, 2026-10-14): the existing sidebar (`_sidebar.html`) is the default for every admin page and never changes structurally -- only its contents change (brand and form lists from `GET /api/v1/admin/metadata/sidebar`). Never create, propose or ask about a different or second sidebar. Also recorded in `docs/API_CONTRACT.md` (Global Rules, "Sidebar rule").
- Shared files: `app/templates/_sidebar.html` (nav), `app/templates/_form_builder.html` (Form Builder modal and created toast), `app/static/admin-sidebar.css` (palette tokens, body, sidebar and form-row styles), `app/static/admin-sidebar.js` (sidebar render via `textContent`, `refreshSidebar()`, 3-dot menu open/close, Form Builder modal save).
- A page sets `{% set active_page = 'dashboard' %}` or `'archive'` before including `_sidebar.html`. New modules add their own value and a matching `{% if %}` in `_sidebar.html`.
- Page wrapper for every module: `div.container-fluid.p-0` > `div.row.g-0` > `_sidebar.html` + `main.col-md-10.px-4.pb-5`. A plain `container-fluid` adds 12px side padding and shifts the page (found on Archive, fixed).
- Page load order: Bootstrap 5.3.0 CSS, Nunito font, `/static/admin-sidebar.css`, page-only CSS; at the end of body: `_form_builder.html`, Bootstrap 5.3.0 bundle, `/static/admin-sidebar.js`, page script. Page scripts call `refreshSidebar()` and must not define their own sidebar loaders.

## Session Log 2026-10-13 (form builder Save: Yes/No questions persisted)
- DONE (schema): migration `migrations/004_job_questions_form_columns.sql` applied to the live DB. Adds `job_questions.form_id` (FK to forms), `question_type`, `options` (JSONB), index `idx_jq_form_order`; relaxes `job_id`, `parameter_id`, `expected_answer` to NULLABLE (no drops, no data touched, table had 0 rows). `form_id` and `question_type` are left NULLABLE on purpose (old job_id based public routes still read this table); the API enforces them. The held plan's later migrations shift up by one: answer_points is now 005, applications.brand_points / answers.is_passed / idx_answers_application_id is 006. Dropping `job_id` and `expected_answer` is still HELD and needs explicit confirmation.
- DONE (backend): `JobQuestion` in `app/models.py` aligned with the table (`options` is `JSONB(none_as_null=True)`, fixing a bug where an omitted value was stored as JSON null instead of SQL NULL; the one affected throwaway row was converted). New schemas `QuestionCreateRequest`, `AnswerPointItem`, `QuestionResponse` in `app/schemas.py`. New route `POST /api/v1/admin/forms/{form_id}/questions` (`create_question` in `app/routers/admin.py`). Verified live (12 cases): 201 YES_NO with default labels and with custom labels (`display_order` 1, 2); 422 for equal labels (case-insensitive), one label, blank text, unknown type, non-empty answer_points, is_hard_requirement true, MULTIPLE_CHOICE with 2 options, malformed form id; 404 unknown form; 409 archived form. NOT tested: MULTIPLE_CHOICE and OPEN_ENDED success paths, an empty option label, an empty options list on YES_NO.
- Interim limits recorded in the contract: until the answer_points table exists, a non-empty `answer_points` or `is_hard_requirement` true returns 422. A question stored with no answer points never fails an applicant and adds no brand points. Known limit: `display_order` is max + 1 with no unique constraint, so two simultaneous saves to one form could share a number.
- DONE (frontend): Form Builder Save in `app/static/admin-sidebar.js` creates the form, then posts each Yes / No card in order (name + two labels as `options`). Saved cards lock; if a question fails after the form exists, Save can be pressed again and retries only the unsaved cards. Other element types (Open ended, Multiple Choice) block Save with a message until they have contracts. Verified in the browser (Network trace: forms 201 then questions 201; DB shows the question stored with its labels). The partial-failure retry path is untested.
- LESSON: the browser cached the old `admin-sidebar.js` after the edit, so early test saves created forms with no questions. Hard-refresh (Ctrl+Shift+R) after JS changes. Consider a cache-busting version on the script tags (not done).
- KNOWN MISMATCH: `docs/SCHEMA.md` says `job_questions.form_id` and `question_type` are NOT NULL; the live table leaves them nullable until the final reshape.
- UPDATE: PATCH and DELETE question endpoints are now implemented (see the form edit session log below); OPEN: Delete from the UI on an existing form; Open ended and Multiple Choice elements; Multiple Choice allowing 2 or more options is undecided (contract says exactly 4); the form view page (per-form Submissions wireframe, scope to confirm); `/admin` vs `/admin/home` mismatch (v4 section 3).

## Session Log 2026-10-14 (form builder: Multiple Choice element, variable-length options)
- DECISION (user, explicit): MULTIPLE_CHOICE moved from the contracted "exactly 4 options, A-D" scheme to a variable-length (minimum 3, no maximum) scheme with no letters shown to applicants; each choice identified internally by a stable, server-assigned id rather than a letter or raw text, so edits don't orphan existing answer_points/applicant answers.
- DONE (contract, before code per 17.2): `docs/API_CONTRACT.md` updated -- create/update question request+response, admin form detail GET, public questions GET, scoring section, admin application detail (applicant_answer resolved to option text for readability). `docs/SCHEMA.md` updated -- `job_questions.options` and `answer_points.answer_value` column comments. No migration needed (`options` is already JSONB).
- DONE (backend): `app/schemas.py` options fields widened to `Optional[List[Any]]`. `app/routers/admin.py`: `create_question` validates MULTIPLE_CHOICE as >=3 distinct options and converts plain text into `{"id","text"}` objects (server-generated uuid4 hex) before storing. `update_question` now branches YES_NO vs MULTIPLE_CHOICE separately for `options`; MULTIPLE_CHOICE PATCH keeps an existing choice's `id` when the client resends it, assigns a new id to any choice sent without one, rejects unknown ids, and (implicitly) drops ids the client stops sending. NOTE left in code: once the `answer_points` table exists, dropped option ids must also delete their `answer_points` rows in the same transaction -- not wired yet since that table isn't live.
- DONE (frontend): `app/templates/_form_builder.html` -- Multiple Choice button now has `data-element-type="MULTIPLE_CHOICE"`; new dynamic choices block (`element-mc-choices-wrap`) with add/remove rows, toggled like the existing Yes/No choices row. `app/static/admin-sidebar.js` -- `openElementEditor` has a third branch; new helpers `mcChoiceRow`/`setMcChoices`/`getMcChoiceValues` back the dynamic list (minimum 3 enforced client-side, remove button no-ops below 3); `editElement` and `openFormBuilderForEdit`'s loaded-question rendering both handle MULTIPLE_CHOICE (populate from `card.dataset.options`, a JSON-stringified `{id,text}` array); the confirm-button handler validates (>=3, non-empty, distinct case-insensitive) and both creates new cards and PATCHes existing ones; Save's per-card payload sends plain option text on create (server assigns ids) and the block-check now allows MULTIPLE_CHOICE through.
- Verified in the browser: modal opens with 3 empty rows + "+ Add choice"; remove refuses below 3; a 5-choice question ("Choose one only": bla/ble/bli/blo/blu) was created, saved, and persisted correctly after a form reopen (toast "Edited Successfully").
- LESSON (repeat of the 2026-10-13 one, same root cause): after editing `admin-sidebar.js`, a hard refresh was needed before the new Multiple Choice click handler took effect -- a session with no console errors and a button that visually does nothing on click is a strong signal to suspect stale cached JS before assuming a code bug. Cache-busting on the script tags is still not done (noted previously, still open).
- NOT YET DONE / OUT OF SCOPE (user decision, explicit): the public apply endpoints (`GET /api/v1/public/jobs/{job_id}/questions`, `POST /apply`) were NOT touched. Two pre-existing gaps found and left as-is: (1) that GET endpoint filters `JobQuestion.job_id == job_id`, but `create_question`/`update_question` only ever set `form_id` on a question (never `job_id`) -- any Form-Builder-created question (of ANY type, not just Multiple Choice) is invisible to this endpoint today. (2) its response (`PublicJobQuestion`) only returns `id`, `question_text`, `display_order` -- no `question_type` or `options` at all, so the apply wizard cannot currently render Yes/No, Multiple Choice, or distinguish Open Ended. - NEW REQUIREMENT (user, explicit, this session): `/apply` becomes a two-step flow instead of one single-page form:
  - **Step 1 (personal data form):** keeps the existing fields (first name, last name, email, phone, resume PDF) and ADDS `address`, `gender`, `date_of_birth`.   - **Step 2 (question wizard):** the job's form questions, fed by the already-implemented `GET /api/v1/public/jobs/{job_id}/questions` (returns `question_type`, `options`, display_order), rendered one at a time -- this activates the apply-wizard layout already decided in the Gem Prompts v4 preamble (section 5) and the wireframe referenced in the 2026-10-12 Decisions Recorded: progress bar ("X/25" style), one question centered, Previous/Next nav.

This decision shapes the request/response shape for both steps and must be settled before implementation starts.

- DECIDED (user, 2026-10-15): submission timing for the two-step apply flow is single-submit -- step 1's personal data (including address/gender/DOB) is held client-side, NOT sent to the backend when "Proceed with questions" is clicked, and is submitted together with the step 2 question answers in one final POST at the end. This matches the existing `POST /api/v1/public/apply` contract shape (multipart, all fields + answers array in one request) rather than requiring a new partial-save endpoint.

The viewer relies on the endpoint not sending `X-Frame-Options` or a frame-blocking CSP; a future header change must keep same-origin framing allowed.

## Session Log 2026-11-06 (part 18): POST /api/v1/admin/change-password IMPLEMENTED (backend only, commit 680a722)
- DECIDED (user, 2026-11-06), each confirmed one at a time: shared password rules live in a helper function called from the handler AFTER the credentials verify (option B, not a schema validator, so no rule feedback leaks before the verify); the 422 detail is ONE string listing every unmet rule (option B).
- IMPLEMENTED (commit 680a722): `POST /api/v1/admin/change-password` (`change_password` in `app/routers/auth.py`, on `auth.router`, so exempt from admin auth); new file `app/password_rules.py` (`validate_new_password`, constants `PASSWORD_MIN_BYTES = 8` and `PASSWORD_MAX_BYTES = 72`); `ChangePasswordRequest` in `app/schemas.py` (`email` EmailStr, two plain strings, no rule validation). Order: limiter, user lookup, generic 401 (unknown email with dummy verify, inactive, wrong temporary password, not flagged; each counted by `record_login_failure`), then rules (422), then hash in `run_in_threadpool`, `must_change_password` = false and the user's sessions rows deleted in one commit, then 204 with no cookie. Backups in ~: auth.py.bak-chpw-20261106, schemas.py.bak-chpw-20261106.
- FINDING (read-only test in the container, ASCII only): bcrypt 4.0.1 with passlib 1.7.4 silently truncates input over 72 bytes (72, 73 and 100 byte inputs all hash and verify). So the 72-byte cap is our own rule, and a long temporary or login password gives a 401, not a 500.
- TESTED, helper: seven cases run directly (valid, too short, no uppercase, three unmet, same as temporary, multibyte 74 bytes flagged on length only, a space is not a special character).
- TESTED by curl (script outside the repo, `~/bte_change_password_test.sh`, prompts read from /dev/tty) on the part 16 RECRUITER test account after a fresh reset by the SUPERADMIN: 401 for an unknown email and for a wrong temporary password (an unknown email with a weak new password still gave 401, so rules are checked only after the verify); 422 for a weak password and for new = temporary; 204 with 0 Set-Cookie headers on a valid change; login with the new password 200 with 1 cookie; login with the old temporary password 401; a change on the now unflagged account 401. A first run failed step 5 with a 422 naming only the uppercase rule (the typed password had none), and a second run hit the shared limiter (429 from step 2), which also confirmed the limiter is shared with login. The third run, after a 65 second wait, passed all eight steps.
- TESTED in the DB (read-only psql): `must_change_password` false, `is_active` true, 1 sessions row (from the later sign-in), `last_login_at` moved only at that sign-in.
- OBSERVED in the browser: a flagged account's login shows 'Something went wrong. Please try again.' (login.html does not handle the 403 yet).
- NOT runtime-tested: sessions deletion on success (code reading only; no count was taken before the sign-in); an inactive flagged account; a flagged account with a wrong temporary password through this endpoint; that a 422 stays out of the limiter (code reading only); multi-byte passwords through the endpoint; the framework 422 shape for malformed fields.
- NOT IMPLEMENTED: wiring of the `/change-password` page, and the `login.html` handling of the 403. A flagged user still cannot sign in through the UI. Do not reset or add real users until both are built.
- UNCONFIRMED ASSUMPTIONS (recorded in the docs): "special character" = any character that is not a letter, number or whitespace; deleting the user's sessions rows on a successful change is safe.
- HARDCODES: `PASSWORD_MIN_BYTES` and `PASSWORD_MAX_BYTES` (the single shared definition); the six rule phrases in `app/password_rules.py`; the generic 401 string duplicated in the new handler (as in login). Seen, not changed: the 5 failures per 60s limiter, 8h SESSION_DURATION_SECONDS, cookie secure=False.
- DOCS UPDATED: `API_CONTRACT.md` (422 wording, bcrypt note, Status line, Last verified; backup bak40-20261106) and this file. `SECURITY.md` and `SCHEMA.md` need no change.
- CLEANUP pending the user's yes naming the file: `~/bte_login403_test.sh` and `~/bte_change_password_test.sh` (both outside the repo, no secrets).
